<input type='checkbox' name='article_1312' value='https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/a-browser-extension-risk-guide-after.html' /> 1312 - <a href='https://www.google.com/search?q=thehackernews.com+A+Browser+Extension+Risk+Guide+After+the+ShadyPanda+Campaign%EE%A0%82Dec+15%2C+2025Browser+Security+%2F+SaaS+SecurityIn+early+December+2025%2C+security+researchers+exposed+a+cybercrime+campaign++that+had+quietly+hijacked+popular+Chrome+and+Edge+browser+extensions+on+a+massive+scale.++A+threat+group+dubbed+ShadyPanda+spent+seven+years+playing+the+long+game%2C+publishing+or+acquiring+harmless+extensions%2C+letting+them+run+clean+for+years+to+build+trust+and+gain+millions+of+installs%2C+then+suddenly+flipping+them+into+malware+via+silent+updates.+In+total%2C+about+4.3+million+users+installed+these+once-legitimate+add-ons%2C+which+suddenly+went+rogue+with+spyware+and+backdoor+capabilities.++This+tactic+was+essentially+a+browser+extension+supply-chain+attack.++The+ShadyPanda+operators+even+earned+featured+and+verified+badges+in+the+official+Chrome+Web+Store+and+Microsoft+Edge+Add-ons+site+for+some+extensions%2C+reinforcing+user+confidence.+Because+extension+updates+happen+automatically+in+the+background%2C+the+attackers+were+able+to+push+out+malicious+code+without+users+noticing+a+thing.++Once+activated+in+mid-2024%2C+the...' target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'>Search - </a> <a href='https://12ft.io/https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/a-browser-extension-risk-guide-after.html' target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'>A Browser Extension Risk Guide After the ShadyPanda CampaignDec 15, 2025Browser Security / SaaS SecurityIn early December 2025, security researchers exposed a cybercrime campaign  that had quietly hijacked popular Chrome and Edge browser extensions on a massive scale.  A threat group dubbed ShadyPanda spent seven years playing the long game, publishing or acquiring harmless extensions, letting them run clean for years to build trust and gain millions of installs, then suddenly flipping them into malware via silent updates. In total, about 4.3 million users installed these once-legitimate add-ons, which suddenly went rogue with spyware and backdoor capabilities.  This tactic was essentially a browser extension supply-chain attack.  The ShadyPanda operators even earned featured and verified badges in the official Chrome Web Store and Microsoft Edge Add-ons site for some extensions, reinforcing user confidence.
